## 4. What is validated, and by what

| claim | established by | status |
|---|---|---|
| the venue settles on a trailing-TWAP comparison — the 60-second feed at the close against the same feed at the open | TZ-04b V2: R1 agrees with the venue's resolved outcome on **200 of 200**, against a 199-of-200 gate fixed before any data was seen; re-run by TZ-06 R-c and reproduced exactly | **stands.** The only artifact here validated against a Polymarket settlement rule. |
| the interval-average reading is wrong, and so is the pre-August snapshot reading | TZ-04b V2: R2 175 of 200, R3 181 of 200; both reproduced by TZ-06 R-c | **stands.** Wrong on one interval in eight and one in ten — not near-misses. |
| the price to beat equals the settlement feed's reading at the open | TZ-04b V1 198 of 200; TZ-06 V3 **396 of 400**, median residual 3.78e-12 USD, worst 25.49 USD | **stands, with a ~1% failure rate every later result carries.** |
| the causal reading is genuinely causal | TZ-01a perturbation test; TZ-06 V4: 140 of 140 bit-identical under perturbation of the entire future, 140 of 140 negative controls move the model output; TZ-07a V2 repeats it on the corrected pricer, 120 of 120 and 120 of 120 | **stands.** |
| the oracle feed supports reconstructing the settlement average | TZ-06 P: the reconstructed close TWAP agrees in sign with the venue's outcome on **397 of 400**, against a floor of 396 fixed before any data was seen | **stands as a precondition.** Median absolute residual against the venue's own report `0.53` USD, worst `11.87`. |
| the Phase 1 pipeline reproduces on a later day and a different process | TZ-07a V4: `tz06-calibration.py` unmodified, six of six `Brier` values reproduced to all six decimals, P at 397 of 400 | **stands** |
| `p_fair` is not disqualified by the TZ-06 gate | TZ-06: G1 6 of 6, `Brier` 0.2107 … 0.0105 against 0.2496; G2 0 failing bins of 34 eligible | **stands as stated and for nothing more.** Phase 1 can only disqualify; it did not. |
| **`p_fair` is overconfident at `tau >= 120`** | Architect's audit of TZ-06's tables, then TZ-07a's own `λ̂`: **1.5220 / 1.4155 / 1.4139** at `tau` 240 / 180 / 120, and 1.1260 / 1.1368 / 1.4027 at 90 / 60 / 30 | **stands as a finding.** In-sample and post-hoc; it closes nothing. |
| **the oracle feed's variance grows faster than the lag** | TZ-07a M1, V1: fifteen lags, `3,028,021` increments, 400 members — `g(20) = 1.9561`, `g(300) = 2.2023` | **stands as a measurement of the feed** and of nothing else. |
| the TZ-07a correction over-widens `sd` below `tau = 120` | Architect's audit of TZ-07a §2.5: corrected `λ̂` 1.0301 / 0.9688 / 0.9846 at 240 / 180 / 120 but 0.8070 / 0.8128 at 90 / 60 and 1.4027 at 30 | **SUPERSEDED by TZ-07b.** `G_RATIO` is out of the pricer; this finding is why. Items 19 and 20 are closed. |
| **the dispersion of the settlement quantity, measured on the variable the `sd` divides** | TZ-07b M1, V1: `1,384,934` anchors of `1,390,800` over the 400 TZ-06 members at seven taus, `Λ` frozen into `pfair.py` as seven literals, each asserted against the measurement before anything was printed | **stands as a measurement of the feed.** Tested out of sample by TZ-08a V8, and it does not transfer — see the rows below. |
| the corrected pricer is causal and the pipeline still reproduces | TZ-07b V2 120 of 120 bit-identical under perturbation and 120 of 120 negative controls; V3 two full runs byte-identical; V4 TZ-06's six `Brier` values to all six decimals with P at 397 of 400, and TZ-07a's fifteen pooled `g(h)` to six significant digits | **stands** |
| **`p_fair` passes G1 and G2 out of sample** | TZ-08a on a disjoint 400: G1 6 of 6, smallest margin `0.047` at `tau = 240`; G2 **0 failing bins of 39 eligible** against an allowance of 2. The uncorrected pricer on the same set fails **7 of 36** | **stands.** Re-derived by the Architect: all 79 eligible bins reproduced in exact integer arithmetic, region and verdict, and the member list rebuilt from the disclosure table to the same SHA-256. It confirms nothing — Phase 1 can only disqualify. |
| **`p_fair` FAILS G3 out of sample, at `tau` 60 and 30** | TZ-08a's scale statistic, judged by the Architect against TZ-07a §8's `\|λ̂ − 1\| <= 0.15`: `λ̂` = 0.8629 / 1.1397 / 1.0291 / 1.0122 / **1.2949** / **2.0418** at 240 / 180 / 120 / 90 / 60 / 30 | **PHASE 1 ANSWERS NO.** §8: any one of G1, G2, G3 violated closes it. 240 and 180 pass with 0.013 and 0.010 to spare, which is not a margin on 400 observations. |
| the pre-registered prediction of item 22 is **refuted** | it predicted 0.999 / 0.946 / 0.945 / **0.761** / **0.787** / 1.012 and a failure at 90 and 60 with `λ̂` below 1. Observed: a pass at 90, and failures at 60 and 30 with `λ̂` above 1 | **the prediction failed, and how it failed is the finding.** It assumed the uncorrected `λ̂` is stable out of sample. It is not: 1.522→1.315, 1.416→1.705, 1.414→1.540, 1.126→1.497, 1.137→**1.871**, 1.403→**2.830** between two adjacent day-and-a-half windows. |
| **the two scale measurements point in opposite directions** | TZ-08a V8 ran the TZ-07b instrument unmodified on the new 400: `Λ_oos / SD_SCALE` = 1.046 / 1.019 / 0.929 / 0.913 / 0.910 / 0.913 at 240 … 30 | **stands as a measurement.** The RMS says narrow the `sd` by ~9% at short `tau`; `λ̂` says widen it by 29% at 60 and by 104% at 30. No constant per `tau` satisfies both: they are two functionals of a distribution that is not the assumed one. |
| **the settlement residual is not Gaussian, and less so the smaller `tau` is** | TZ-08a V9 on the same 400: `P(\|r\| > 3Λ)` = 0.0125 / 0.0085 / 0.0106 / 0.0158 / 0.0219 / 0.0268 / 0.0253 at 240 … 10, against the normal's 0.0027 — **8 to 10 times the normal tail below `tau = 90`** — while `MAD / 0.674490` sits 18 / 22 / 30 / 41 / 56 / 71 / 81 per cent below `Λ` | **stands, and it is why G3 failed.** A sharp centre with a heavy tail is not a normal at any scale. Item 22 suspected this in sample; out of sample the gap is roughly twice as wide. |
| **`λ̂` at `tau = 30` is carried by one observation** | Architect's arithmetic over TZ-08a §11 item 1: at `T0 1789268400` the feed moved about 40 USD in the final 30 s, `z = −10.819`, `p_fair` exactly `0.0`, and the venue resolved Up. That single point gains **+45.2** log points between `λ = 1` and `λ̂ = 2.0418`; the other 399 **lose 14.7** | **stands as a finding about the estimator and about the tail.** `Φ` assigned about 1e-27 to something that happened once in 400. Without that point the maximiser is below 1 — which does not rescue the gate, and is not a reason to drop it. |
| **the heavy tail is not a lagging `sigma`** | TZ-10b M1 and its §4.1 reading: `sigma_post`, the successor interval's realised scale, leaves `κ` higher at 7 of 7 `tau`. `f`, the share of the excess it removes, is **−1.3793** at `tau = 60` and **−0.9437** at 30; re-derived by the Architect at 7 of 7 | **stands.** The pre-registered prediction was 0.5–0.7 and 0.6–0.8. It failed, and the sign is the finding. |
| **conditioning on the realised scale error does not remove it either** | TZ-10b M3: within deciles of `log(sigma_post / sigma_hat)` the size-weighted `κ` is at or above pooled at 5 of 7 `tau`; the extreme deciles read `3.449` and `2.655` at `tau = 30` against `1.9753` pooled | **stands, and this is what closes the question.** Not M1 and not `f`: this is the test a scale mixture must pass and does not. |
| **the scale error is not forecastable from causal inputs** | TZ-10b M4: four causal regressors, fitted on the intersection's TZ-06 share and evaluated on its TZ-08a share against the in-sample mean — `R²` `0.1090` in sample, **`−0.0189`** out | **stands.** No model adopted, and this closes the repair-by-better-`sigma` route for those regressors. |
| **the heavy tail sits in the low-`sigma_hat` deciles** | TZ-10b M3, causal stratifier: `κ` `3.227` in decile 1 against `1.119` in decile 10 at `tau = 30`; `2.611` against `1.077` at 60 | **stands, and it is observable at decision time.** TZ-11 turns it into an admissibility rule. |
| the TZ-10b instrument is causal and reproduces what it must | V2 140 of 140 bit-identical and 140 of 140 controls, perturbed strictly after the checkpoint; V4 TZ-07b's `Λ` at 7 of 7 and TZ-08a V8's six ratios at 6 of 6; V3 three outputs byte-identical over two runs; V6 `SD_SCALE`, `corrected_sd`, `phi` and `state` byte-identical | **stands.** All three member lists rebuilt by the Architect from the report's own disclosure: 3 of 3. Nothing was re-fitted. |
| **`g`, TZ-10b §4.2's second reading, is not evidence** | Architect's audit: its denominator is `\|λ̂ − 1\|`, which is `0.0013` at `tau = 120` and sends `g` to `−122`; at `tau = 30` it reads `0.2115` against a `0.20` boundary and becomes `0.6574` once the single most influential observation is removed | **WITHDRAWN as a reading.** The verdict rests on `f` and on M3, both anchor-level. See §7 item 40. |
| **the settlement residual is Student-shaped, and within the domain the shape transfers from weekday to weekday** | TZ-11a M1 and M3: the Student `t` beats the best normal at 42 of 42 fits; on test 2's admissible members `\|log(ν̂ / LINK_NU)\| <= log 2` at 7 of 7 `tau` and `κ` within `0.041` of the fit set's | **stands as a measurement of the feed**, over 83,000 to 139,000 anchors per `tau` on test 2. Test 2's admissible rows are 85% Monday: weekday to weekday is all it shows. |
| **the Student pricer is closed by its own gate** | TZ-11a §4: G1 7 of 7 on both sets; G2 0 failing on both — **of 0 eligible on test 1**; G3 fails at `tau` 240, 180, 30, 10 on test 1 and at 90, 60 on test 2; G4 fails at 120, 90, 60, 30 on test 1. Re-derived by the Architect: 110 eligible bins in exact integers, every G1, G3 and G4 comparison, and M5 to the last digit | **stands as the verdict TZ-11a §4 fixed, and is not re-judged.** The pre-registered prediction was right on the answer and wrong on the mechanism: G4 held at `tau = 240` on both sets and failed at mid and short `tau` on test 1. |
| **G3, as written, cannot tell a calibrated pricer from a miscalibrated one on these populations** | Architect's audit: labels drawn from the pricer's own `p_t`, the report's bin tables standing in for its rows — an approximation that reproduces the reported `λ̂` to within `0.12` — at 4,000 replicates per population. With the pricer exactly right, G3 fails a given `tau` with probability `0.19` to `0.35` at `tau` 240 … 60, `0.52` at 30 and `0.71` at 10 on test 2's admissible rows, `0.67` to `1.00` on test 1's, and `0.09` to `0.74` even over all 400; some `tau` fails with probability at least `0.71` on every population scored. On test 2 the seven Student likelihood-ratio p-values run `0.019` to `0.85` | **stands as a finding about the gate, not about the pricer.** The TZ-11a closure is procedural: it shows the Student pricer neither miscalibrated nor calibrated. TZ-12 measures the same thing exactly. See §7 item 49. |
| **the gate's own error rates, measured exactly** | TZ-12 M2: 20,000 null replicates per population, labels drawn from the frozen pricer's own `p_t`, 14 populations. Old G3 fails an exactly correct pricer at `0.345` / `0.247` / `0.224` / `0.259` / `0.337` / `0.620` / `0.703` at `tau` 240 … 10 on test 2's admissible rows and at `0.684` to `1.000` on test 1's; some `tau` fails with probability at least `0.703`, and `0.979` under independence across `tau`. Old G1 fails at `0.0985` at `tau = 240` on test 1 and at 0 of 20,000 everywhere on test 2. Old G2 has 0 eligible bins at every gated `tau` on test 1 | **stands, and it closes the measurement half of §7 item 49.** Re-derived by the Architect from the report's own null tables: every `k2` of 7, the six-`tau` convolution `0.000080`, `1 − Π(1 − f)` = `0.9788`, and the 24 eligible bins equal TZ-11a's committed 24. The audit approximation that raised item 49 is confirmed — it said 0.19–0.35 at `tau` 240 … 60 and about 0.71 at 10 against 0.224–0.345 and 0.703 measured, and understated `tau = 30` (0.52 against 0.620). |
| **why the old G3 failed: the scale estimate runs to the edge** | TZ-12's diagnostic: the null `λ̂_grid` sits on the grid's lower bound `0.5` in `43%` of replicates at `tau = 10` on test 2 and `96%` on test 1, against 0 at `tau` 240 … 90 on test 2 | **stands.** With no confident prediction contradicted there is nothing to hold the maximiser, which is the mechanism item 49 named and this measures. |
| **TZ-11a's G4 failures on the weekend at `tau` 60 and 30 survive sizing** | TZ-12 M2's normal approximation to the old G4: false-failure `0.6279` / `0.5304` / `0.5629` / `0.3760` / **`0.0171`** / **`0.0002`** / `0.0000` at `tau` 240 … 10 on test 1. Re-derived by the Architect at 14 of 14, and the bootstrap has no bound hit at 60 or 30, so the spread constant there is uncensored | **stands as the one TZ-11a gate verdict that is signal rather than gate noise.** The tail index did not transfer to the weekend at short `tau`. The same TZ's G4 failures at 120 and 90 are not evidence: a correct pricer fails there 56% and 38% of the time. |
| **the sized gate admits at most two horizons on a 400-slot set** | TZ-12 M3 on test 2's 231 to 237 admissible members: G3's power against a 1.5× overconfidence is `0.2780` / `0.4990` / `0.6255` / `0.6180` / `0.4785` / `0.1645` / `0.0180` at `tau` 240 … 10, so only 120 and 90 clear the `0.5` floor. G1 could pass at 7 of 7, `P0` 0 of 20,000. G4 has power only at 60 and 30; `tau = 10` is censored and reads UNDECIDABLE | **stands, and it is why test 3 is 750 slots and not 400.** All 14 `c4`, 14 `s` and 14 `P4` re-derived by the Architect, and `Z4` = `Φ⁻¹(1 − 0.00125)` and `U_BOOT` = `sqrt(23 / χ²₀.₁₀(23))` to 15 digits; the family-wise Bonferroni total is `0.048` exactly. At 2,000 power replicates `tau` 180 and 60 lie inside one Monte Carlo standard error of the floor (§7 item 56). |
| **`SIGMA_LOG_NU`, the member-bootstrap spread of `log ν̂`, on the fit set alone** | TZ-12 M1: 24 refits of the 280 admissible fit members per `tau` — `0.476750` / `0.338445` / `0.361253` / `0.236061` / `0.0876792` / `0.0557673` / `0.0326229`, `tau = 10` censored at 7 of 24 refits on the search's lower bound | **stands as a measurement of the fit, with one caveat re-derived by the Architect:** at `tau` 240 and 120 the largest replicate sits on the search's **upper** bound `60` and carries `53%` and `33%` of the variance, which inflates the constant and widens G4 where G4 has no power anyway (§7 item 57). No verdict moves either way. |
| **test 1 is the weekend, and the absolute domain rule turns the pricer off there** | the Architect's reading of TZ-11a's disclosure against the calendar: median `sigma_hat` `0.92` USD/s on Saturday against `2.56` to `2.96` on Thursday, Friday and Monday; `ADMIT` admits 6% to 8% of weekend intervals | **stands as a fact about the three sets.** That the regime rather than the dates drives the non-transfer of every earlier constant is a hypothesis, not a measurement. |
| **the Student pricer is NOT DISQUALIFIED at `tau` 240, 180, 120, 90 and 60 under the sized gate** | TZ-13, applied once to test 3's 514 to 528 admissible members per `tau` — a Tuesday–Thursday set no one had scored — with every constant on disk before any outcome was read: G1 PASS at 7 of 7, `P0` 0 of 20,000 and `P1` 1.0000; G2 0 failing bins at every `tau` against `k2` 2 or 3; G3's likelihood ratio at most `6.230197` against `crit3` `7.087974` to `9.504963`, PASS with `P3` 0.6890 / 0.9295 / 0.9755 / 0.9675 / 0.9090 at 240 … 60; no G4 FAIL anywhere; §4.3's band at 0 of 21. `tau` 30 and 10 read UNDECIDABLE on G3's power, 0.3230 and 0.0495. Re-derived by the Architect: every `k2` from the null tables, all seven `s`, `c4` and `P4` from `SIGMA_LOG_NU`, `Z4` and `U_BOOT` at 40 digits, every gate reading and the verdict at 7 of 7 | **stands — Phase 1 does not disqualify the Student pricer at five `tau`, inside its domain, on weekday data.** It confirms nothing: Phase 1 can only disqualify. It admits those five `tau` to Phase 2 and to nothing else. |
| **at `tau` 90, 60 and 30 the Student pricer leans under-confident on test 3** | TZ-13: `λ̂` 0.8844 / 0.7436 / 0.6855 under the Student log CDF, likelihood ratios 1.906 / 6.230 / 3.135 — asymptotic χ²₁ p-values 0.17 / 0.013 / 0.077, none near the family bound — and 0.8559 / 0.6815 / 0.5979 without the single most influential member. G3's power against λ = 1/1.5 is 0.6500 at 60 and 0.0850 at 30. The residual scale points the other way: `ŝ` sits 2.5% to 3.1% above `LINK_SCALE` at 90 … 30 | **stands as a finding, not a failure.** The probability scale and the residual scale disagree in direction at short `tau`, as the normal link's did in TZ-08a V8, and which one describes the sign of the outcome is not measured. **A market quoting more confidently than `p_t` at these `tau` is not, by that fact, mispricing.** |
| **the Student shape holds on a third weekday set at `tau <= 120`** | TZ-13 on test 3's admissible members: `\|log(ν̂ / LINK_NU)\|` 0.076 / 0.061 / 0.088 / 0.093 / 0.034 at `tau` 120 … 10, every fit interior, and `ŝ` within 3.1% of `LINK_SCALE` there. At 240 and 180 `ν̂` reads 30.0 and 37.0 against 9.62 and 13.7, and dropping one member sends it to the search's upper bound 60 at 240 | **stands as a measurement of the feed.** At long `tau` the residual is near-normal and `ν` is not identified; G4 has had no power there on any set read. |
| **what the sized gate sees on 750 weekday slots** | TZ-13 §3.3 and §3.6: G3's power reaches the 0.5 floor at `tau` 240 … 60 and not at 30 or 10; G4's `P4` is below it at 240 … 90 at every multiple to 3×. quote withdrawn on Binance's adverse move (§5, What TZ-32 found) — and read RTDS live for ten seconds (§6). Every one of
these is read, and no figure of the exploration is evidence. **No fill of a five-minute market with `T0 > 1792753800`
has been read by anyone; no settled document of a leg after `btc-updown-5m-1793023800`, the last of the 300 TZ-32's
fetch requested; no stream or stored book of a window after `1793013300`; and no Binance trade after 2026-10-26
23:59:59 UTC.** **For the L track, on 2026-10-27, the Architect** read the venue's reward configurations —
`clob.polymarket.com/rewards/markets/current` and `/sampling-simplified-markets`, 14,089 and 14,092 rows — the order
books of the first token of 1,726 of those markets, the documents of 1,723 of them through gamma `/markets`, and the
venue's fee, liquidity-rewards and holding-rewards pages. All of it framed the track and is excluded from L0's sets by
time (CANON PART II). **For TZ-33, on 2026-10-27, the Architect** ran TZ-33's instrument against the venue three times,
from 14:45, 17:47 and 18:33 UTC, on a session's machine — three selections of 150 markets for that day and 15 whole
slots of their streams and reward polls — and **on 2026-10-28 its audit of TZ-33** read the gamma and CLOB documents of
P-START's two markets, neither a member, for their end and their tags. All of it precedes the reward book's store or
reads no member, and is excluded from L0's sets by time.

**This project has a settlement variable measured and confirmed, a pricer that Phase 1 has not
disqualified at five `tau` inside its domain — which confirms nothing — NO EDGE for a taker against the market at
`tau` 180, 120, 90 and 60, and `tau` 240 undecided and final, its second look withdrawn; NO EARN for a maker inside the
window over both looks — the makers' whole margin is the rebate, under half a tick a share; the nested pair violated
on stored books (B2) but not capturable by a taker through the venue's delay (B3, NO CAPTURE); and the W track's W0
read W-EARN — the ten named makers kept `0.828` c a share, rebate included, on markets no one had read (TZ-31) — and
its W1 read W1-NO-EARN: a quote of ours at the touch keeps under half a tick a share even first in every queue, all of it
rebate, and loses behind it (TZ-32); the W track is closed; and the L track's capture, the reward book, runs since
2026-10-28 (TZ-33).** The
normal link is disqualified at `tau` 60 and 30, and TZ-10b showed why: the settlement residual is not
normal, and less so the smaller `tau` is. TZ-11a fitted the Student link and its causal domain on the
Thursday–Friday set and froze them. TZ-12 measured, from the frozen pricer's own predictions, each
gate's false-failure probability and power, and fixed the sized gate — `0.048` family-wise, power
against a 1.5× overconfidence, UNDECIDABLE without it. TZ-13 applied it once to 750 weekday slots no
one had scored, every constant on disk before any outcome was read. No gate failed anywhere.
**TZ-15 then applied Phase 2's first gate once, and it decided nothing; TZ-16a's first look, on 2,400
slots no one had seen, closed four `tau` of five: NO EDGE.** **Beside the phases, the
backup track has its structure measured and its first prices read:** one published reading settles
every market at a shared boundary, exactly; and B2, TZ-24, read the books the chain book stored from 2026-09-23
17:39:50 UTC to the window of M0's last member and found the containment broken on executable quotes, after both
fees, at 7 of 1,079 windows — VIOLATION, about 1.9 USDC at the touch over eleven days. **A violation measured is not
an edge captured:** TZ-30 measured B3 on the pair book's streams — NO CAPTURE.

**What Phase 2 inherits.** Five `tau`, not seven. A domain that quotes on about 70% of weekday
intervals and 6% to 8% of weekend ones, and no set scored under the sized gate that contains a weekend. A pricer that
leans under-confident at `tau` 90, 60 and 30 on the one set that scored it (§4), so a disagreement
between the market and `p_t` there is not, by itself, the market's error. And the gate discipline of
CANON PART II: a threshold fixed before any quote is aggregated, and a false-failure probability and a
power stated before any outcome the gate judges is read.

**What TZ-15 found.** At each admissible checkpoint of TZ-14's 1,200 it took, on paper, one share of the
side whose executable ask `p_t` called cheap after the taker fee — 1,924 trades over five `tau` — and
asked the venue's outcomes whether that side won more often than the ask it paid implied. It did not:
`S` fell 9 to 28 wins short of the critical count at every `tau`, and below the ask's own expectation
at four of five. No `tau` reads EDGE. None could read NO EDGE either: its only route was power, and the
power to see the pricer's own claimed edge — `0.036` to `0.043` a share above the ask — was `0.33` to
`0.47` at 1,200 slots, fixed before any label was read. After the fact, and barred from every reading,
the outcomes tracked the market's prices rather than the pricer's at four of five `tau`, and the
market's mid beat `p_t` on the Brier score at all five (§4). **The gate's one-sidedness is the
Architect's defect (§7 item 69), and no reading is re-judged.**

**What TZ-16a found.** On the first 2,400 qualifying slots after `1789669800` — Thursday 2026-09-17 18:35
UTC to Saturday 2026-09-26 19:50, 762 of them weekend — it applied TZ-15's selection, fee and domain
unchanged, `641` to `1,058` trades a `tau`, and read each answer from its own exact test at `0.002` a
`tau`, both laws widened by the serial dependence TZ-15's labelled residuals carry. At `tau` 180, 120, 90
and 60 the chosen side won `547`, `506`, `418` and `256` times, below `d` = `559`, `510`, `432` and `273`:
the pricer's claimed edge — `0.040` to `0.048` a share over the ask, `0.032` to `0.041` after the fee — is
rejected, and the side won about as often as its ask implied, `T` from `−0.021` to `+0.003` a share before
the fee and `−0.006` to `−0.028` after it. At 240 it won `538`, ten above `d` and 28 short of `c`: `+0.013`
a share over the ask before the fee and `−0.0007` after it. The dependence moved `c` up and `d` down by one
to four wins at the four `tau` where `f > 1`, and no reading rests on a margin that small: the closest,
120, four wins inside `d`, holds with its single most influential member removed. The market's mid beat
`p_t` on the Brier score at all five `tau` again (§4).

**The second look is withdrawn, 2026-10-04 (CANON §1.5).** TZ-16a §4.2 fixed it — `tau` 240 alone, the first
3,600 qualifying slots, `α2 = β2 = 0.008` — and TZ-19's readiness read found its inputs complete: `3,885` complete
manifests, the 3,624th at `1790853000`. It is not run: even its first look's best figure after the fee,
`−0.0007` a share, leaves nothing for a taker to capture, and its span is worth more to the maker and backup
tracks than to a formal closure.
**What TZ-23 found.** On the first 4,032 qualifying five-minute markets from 2026-09-04 14:00 UTC, past every span
whose outcomes a TZ had read, the makers' side of `4,820,537` taker fills inside `[T0, T0 + 240)` ended at settlement
at `0.211` c a share: `−0.013` c on the fills and `+0.224` c of rebate. Neither bound was reached, at a power of
`0.947` against half a tick. Before the rebate the makers' fills were priced about fairly; their whole margin is the
rebate, which is what M1 would have asked of our own quotes after adverse selection. The second look decided, at no
extra error: NO EARN (TZ-29, below).
**What TZ-24 found.** At each of the seven checkpoints of every window the chain book stored up to M0's last member,
it priced the nested pair that pays at least 1 a share at settlement whatever the price does — Up on one market and
Down on the other, chosen by the sign of `D` — at its two executable asks at the venue's minimum size, after both
legs' taker fees. Over 1,079 windows and 7,553 units, 7 windows offered it below 1 a share with the fee collected
either way and both books read within 150 ms of one another: `0.044` to `0.79` c a share on `8.98` to `118.83`
shares, five of the seven in the wings with one leg at 0.01 to 0.10. Barred from the reading, the best of them a
window would have paid `1.885` USDC at the touch in eleven days. **What stored books cannot say is whether a taker
could have had it:** the 150 ms delay, in which a maker can cancel the stale quote; the depth beyond the level; both
legs filled together; and how long each episode lasted between snapshots 20 to 60 s apart. That is B3's.
**What TZ-26 established.** The pair book runs as a system unit outside every session, in place of the chain book,
which TZ-26 stopped and disabled. On its first four qualifying windows, a Saturday evening, it streamed every frame of
the four books with no gap above `1.953` s and one reconnect, kept 28 of 28 checkpoints complete, left Tier C intact
at its four exposed intervals, used `0.0174` of the one core, and was restarted by systemd `5.480` s after a stray
`SIGTERM`. Its streams fill the store at about `1.22` GB a day, half the `2.52` modelled, so its cap stops them
about six and a half days after its start at that rate and sooner on weekdays; and its sum of them is taken once at
a process's start, which is why TZ-27 restarts it after moving them.
**What TZ-27 established.** The streams can leave the host byte for byte and the unit's count of them be reset with no
window lost: 51 files, 678.7 MB, each proved by GitHub's own digest and a full download before its removal, the unit
restarted inside its idle stretch and its next window streamed. Its upload and verify ran while window `1791706500`
streamed, and that window streamed in two segments, one reconnect — not proven caused, and the reason TZ-28 holds
every request to the idle stretch.
**What TZ-28 established.** The archive runs itself: a oneshot under a daily timer, its first run fired by the timer
600 s after B-ENABLE, which moved 28 files, 430.0 MB, proved each by the Release's digest and a full download before
its removal, restarted the pair book inside its band with its count of the store reset to the store's, and made every
one of its 61 requests inside the idle stretch; its first calendar run moved 46 files, 794.5 MB, the next day. A
window streamed in two segments with no request inside its stream, so the idle stretch does not remove every
reconnect, and B3's measurement reads every window's segments (§7 item 113). Every boot fires one run 600 s after the
timer starts, which doubles as a catch-up; a pair book not `active` at that run's restart halts the archive.
**What TZ-29 found.** On the next 4,032 qualifying markets, 2026-10-05 23:15 UTC to 2026-10-19 23:10 UTC, every one
of them qualifying, TZ-23's sum carried over both looks fell below NO EARN's bound: on `9,704,214` window fills and
`250,976,528` shares the makers kept `0.231` c a share, `0.219` c of it rebate and `0.012` c at settlement — about
nothing before the rebate, as on the first look, and under half a tick a share with it. EARN would have needed the
venue to pay more than `0.2208` of the fee as rebate; it pays `0.2`, measured against its payouts (§4). The maker
track closes for its window, and re-opens only on a change in the maker's terms, on markets no TZ has read (CANON
§1.6).
**What TZ-30 found.** On the first 768 qualifying windows of the pair book's streams, 2026-10-11 08:15 UTC to 2026-10-19
09:45 UTC, every frame replayed at venue time, buying both legs of each firm violation the host saw — 532 attempts in
75 windows — through a one-way latency of 50 ms and the venue's 150 ms delay lost in every block of 96: `−596.06` USDC in
all, where the same attempts were worth `+666.78` at the touch when seen. Removing the delay or zeroing the latency
rescued no variant. The violations are on the book, and gone, on one leg at least, by the time a taker's order
matches: the stale side is moved or taken inside the delay. The backup track closes, and the W track asks which makers
keep an edge on the fills they get.
**What TZ-32 found.** On the first 288 qualifying windows from 2026-10-23 11:15 UTC, five-minute legs no one had read, a
bid and an ask of five shares at the touch, replaced on every move, replayed through the 150 ms taker delay and a 50 ms
latency: first in every queue the quotes kept `0.079` c a share, `−0.185` c at settlement and `0.264` c of rebate, and at
the back of every queue they lost `1.169` c. The settlement part was negative in every variant; zero latency first in
every queue kept `0.151` c, all of it rebate. **The touch quote's fills are adversely selected, and neither speed nor
queue place changes the sign of its settlement.** The Architect's exploration after the reading (above), in-sample and
barred, withdrew a quote when Binance's price moved against it — the perpetual's trades known 125 ms after each, a move
of `0.50` USD over 3 s, the side not quoted again for 1 s — and it lifted every variant without changing the conclusion:
first in every queue `0.337` c a share, `0.379` at zero latency; with departures in proportion `−0.496` c, at the
back of every queue `−0.640` c, `−0.279` at zero latency. The fills worth keeping are the front of the queue's, and
a new quote joins behind the displayed size. W2 does not open, and the W track closes.
**What TZ-33 established.** The reward book runs as a system unit outside every session, beside the recorder and the
pair book: each UTC day it selects 150 reward markets by a rule fixed before their data, every reply it read stored so
that the replies alone re-derive the set; on its first four qualifying slots, a Wednesday midday, it streamed every frame
of their 300 books and trades with no gap above `1.410` s and polled each market's reward record three times a slot,
1,800 of 1,800 served; it used `0.0262` of the core, left the recorder's intervals and the pair book's windows intact,
and was restarted by systemd `5.831` s after a stray `SIGTERM`. Its streams fill its store at about `1.02` GB a day, so
its stream floor would have bound at about 20:00 UTC on 2026-10-30 and its cap at about 10:00 UTC on 2026-11-01.
**TZ-34 moved them:** since 2026-10-29 the reward book's archive moves its closed slots' streams to a Release every day,
unattended — its first run 86 files, `890,975,956` bytes, each proved by the Release's digest and a full anonymous
download before its removal, every removal while the reward book was quiet (§2.6) — and L0's measurement is this
track's next TZ.
**Other assets' markets are not a shortcut:** the pricer's domain threshold is in USD/s and its link
tables are the BTC feed's, so each asset would need its own Phase 0 and Phase 1, and same-window
outcomes across assets move together, so they add far less information than their count.

| item | value |
|---|---|
| host | Vultr VPS (Warsaw); the Executor runs Claude Code CLI **on the VPS itself**. A cloud Claude Code session is not this host and cannot execute anything that touches the capture. |

All three, or it is not the capture host. **Whether a capture process runs is not identity:** it is a state each host gate states for itself (§7 item 86). |
| language | Python 3.12. **The research interpreter is `/root/tz01-env/venv/bin/python`** — Python 3.12.3 with `numpy` 2.5.3 — on which TZ-11a ran; the system `python3` has no `numpy`. The recorder runs from `/root/tz04a-env/` — Python 3.12.3 with `websockets` 17.1, read by TZ-20's and TZ-21's H8 — and so does TZ-26's pair book; TZ-27's instrument needs its standard library alone. Neither environment is scratch and no TZ removes either. |

The archive's uploads under the same token were served that morning. **TZ-33's push, on 2026-10-28 at 06:13 UTC, went through with the URL's own credential**; whether the redirect has ended is not measured, and a TZ keeps `gh`'s route as its fallback; **TZ-34's push, on 2026-10-29 at 07:46 UTC, went through the URL's own credential** as well |

It is the rate of change of free space over a stated window, as §7 item 25 requires, net of each measuring run's own tree, as §7 item 29 requires. **It is conditional on `telemetry-watch.service` staying disabled:** if it is re-enabled the measured active drain of `2,361,691,000` bytes/day returns and headroom is `4.7` days, so every TZ re-reads `df`, `du` and `systemctl is-enabled` in its own §0 and states all three. **The pair book's streams are a drain of their own, measured by TZ-26:** `1,224,503,448` bytes a day at `488.4` frames a second over four windows of a Saturday evening, against `2.52` GB a day modelled at `1,032` frames a second; its other writes `2,363,002` bytes a day; and everything else on the host about `43.5` MB a day across TZ-26 — `2,044,679` bytes over its `4,063` s — and `187,760,570` at the fastest window above. | **price to beat** | published by Gamma only after settlement, at `events[0].eventMetadata.priceToBeat`, and captured per interval in `resolution.json`. Not available before the close; a live pricer reconstructs it from the feed. **Beside it, `finalPrice` is the reading the market settled on, and it equals the next market's `priceToBeat` as the same literal** — 600 of 600 (§4) — so a label can be read from either document. |
| **resolution endpoint** | `GET https://gamma-api.polymarket.com/markets/slug/btc-updown-5m-{T0}`, polled from `T0+333` every 15 s; the same path serves `btc-updown-15m-{T}` (TZ-17, 200 of 200). **A settled document's readings are stable, and its bytes are not:** the 801 TZ-17 stored on 2026-09-22 hashed equal at 801 of 801 on 2026-09-23, but on 2026-10-09 the 400 of TZ-17's 200 windows hash equal at 0 of 400, while every `priceToBeat`, `finalPrice` and outcome equals TZ-17's CSV at 200 of 200 windows (§2.5, §7 item 100). A stored body is re-verified from the venue by its readings, never by its hash. `/markets?slug=…&closed=true` serves the same readings for twenty slugs in one request: TZ-24's rehearsal, run by the Architect, read them so at 200 of 200 windows. |
| **CLOB websocket — the market channel, verified 2026-10-10** | `wss://ws-subscriptions-clob.polymarket.com/ws/market`, public, no credential, read by the Architect from a second host on the ETH nested pairs of that day and, by counts of frames and event types alone, on `btc-updown-15m`; no five-minute BTC market was opened (§5). **The handshake** is answered `101`, with no extension where none is offered, under a non-default `User-Agent`. **The subscribe frame** is `{"assets_ids": [...], "type": "market"}`; the text frame `PING`, sent every 10 s, is answered `PONG`. **After the subscribe** one list frame holds a `book` of every subscribed token — 124 ms later in the window measured, and for markets whose window had not yet opened — then four event types and no other: `book`, `price_change`, `last_trade_price` and `tick_size_change`. Every `price_change` frame names one market and carries one entry for each of its two tokens; a token subscribed alone receives its market's `price_change` frames, both entries, and only its own `book` frames. A five-minute market's document and `/book` are served about a day before its window opens. **Rates:** the ETH pair `115` to `336` frames a second over whole windows inside `[T+595, T+905]`, `btc-updown-15m` alone `140.5`; at gzip level 9, `75` to `82` bytes a frame stored, about `620` raw. **Processor:** `148` to `226` µs a frame on the live ETH pair, REST reads included, and `171` storing 1,000 frames a second on loopback, under Python 3.12.3 with `websockets` 17.1. Perishable venue mechanics: TZ-26's P-START re-read them on the host before its service started, served. **Measured on the host by TZ-26**, over four windows of `btc-updown-15m-{T}` and `btc-updown-5m-{T+600}` on 2026-10-10, a Saturday evening: `488.4` frames a second — `214.8` and `273.6` — `360.5` to `556.3` a window, `83.4` to `85.6` stored bytes a frame at gzip level 9; the venue-to-host lag of `price_change` frames a median of `11` to `23` ms and a 99th percentile of `546`, `46`, `278` and `2,246` ms, the last with a maximum of `2,370`. `recv_ns` is stamped when the event loop takes the frame, so a host-side stall — one processor, the pair book's anon partly swapped, `VmSwap` `12,256` kB — cannot be told from venue batching, and B3's measurement reads the lag's distribution first. **Read offline by the Architect for TZ-30 on 2026-10-20**, on TZ-27's 51 windows — exploration, excluded from B3's set: the pair book stores each frame's `raw` as the venue sent it, a `price_change` in the venue's own key order `market`, `price_changes`, `timestamp`, `event_type`, its two entries each `asset_id`, `price`, `size`, `side`, `hash`, `best_bid`, `best_ask`, with `", "` between items; `best_ask` `"1"` and `best_bid` `"0"` stand for an empty side; prices carry at most three decimals and sizes two. TZ-30's lag — a `price_change` frame's receipt less its venue stamp — over 48 of those windows, `7,227,390` frames: the median window's median `17` ms and its 99th percentile `182` ms, the 99th percentile above `1,000` ms at 8 windows and `5,201` ms at the worst, the longest `5,277` ms. On the ETH nested pair of window `1792489500`, captured live on 2026-10-20 beside its `/book` replies, the book rebuilt from the stream equalled the reply at the touch at 300 of 300 |

| **venue constants, measured TZ-05a and TZ-14** | **tick size:** `0.01` in all three sources at the one instant TZ-05a read them — `orderPriceMinTickSize` (Gamma), `tick_size` (`/book`) and `minimum_tick_size` (`/markets/{condition_id}`) — **but not a constant of the interval**: TZ-14 read `/book` `tick_size` `0.001` at 1,160 of 16,800 replies, while `gamma.json` at `T0 + 5` carries `0.001` at 2 of 1,200 members, so the tick falls to `0.001` inside the interval as the price reaches the wings · **minimum order size `5`**, in every `/book` reply of TZ-14's 16,800 · **fee schedule** `{"exponent": 1, "rate": 0.07, "rebateRate": 0.2, "takerOnly": true}`, `feeType` `crypto_fees_v2`, confirmed by TZ-14 in all 1,200 `gamma.json` · outcome mapping resolved from the outcome strings, `outcomes[i] == "Up"` against `clobTokenIds[i]`, never from position — `["Up", "Down"]` at 1,200 of 1,200 · **how the taker fee is collected** — in USDC beside the price, or in shares out of those bought — is read by no TZ; TZ-24 classifies a violation under both |
| **taker delay, crypto** | `150` ms since 2026-09-04 14:00 UTC, up from `50` ms (2026-08-17) and `250` ms before — the venue's changelog, read by the Architect on 2026-10-04, 2026-10-09, 2026-10-19, 2026-10-20, 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-23, which records no fee, rebate, delay, websocket, order-type, minimum-size or tick change after it through its entries of 2026-10-21; measured by no TZ (§7 item 87). **The venue's order-lifecycle page, read 2026-10-23:** a marketable order on these markets is held, then validated again and matched or placed; during the hold it "is pending and cannot be canceled"; a post-only order that would match immediately "is rejected instead of executed"; the page still gives the hold as `250` ms, and the changelog's later entry governs. TZ-30's replay took the delay as a constant of the venue for its own orders; TZ-32's takes it as the hold before an order that would cross a resting quote of ours matches it |
| **public fills — Data API v2, measured 2026-10-05 and 2026-10-09** | `GET https://data-api.polymarket.com/v2/trades?condition=…&taker_only=…&limit=1000`, no credentials, at most 20 condition ids a request and 1,000 rows a page. `taker_only=true`, the default, serves each fill once on its taker side — one row per taker order and transaction, `size` its shares and `price` the size-weighted mean of its fills — and `false` adds the maker rows; rows under `0.01` share are never served; sizes and prices are JSON numbers with a decimal point, `100.0`. **The cursor carries no filter:** `next_cursor` encodes a page size and a block-time and sequence anchor, and sent alone continues the venue-wide feed, so every request re-sends `condition` and `taker_only` beside it; sent so, 20 markets' rows equal v1's `/trades` by offset paging, 23,588 of 23,588 taker rows and 71,908 of 71,908 with maker rows. `/v2/trades` takes 300 requests in 10 s, gamma `/markets` 300; `/markets?slug=…&slug=…&closed=true` serves up to twenty documents in one request, and `closed` defaults to `false`. **v1 is retired on 2026-10-24**, and its offset stops at 10,000 rows. The changelog's entry of 2026-10-07 changes `/v2/trades` only for requests that carry `user`. Read on markets no TZ scores (§5). **TZ-23's fetch, served at every request:** `1,565` s for 202 chunks and `6,013,558` rows, against `1,789` s projected after 30 chunks; its walk `23.0` s for 202 gamma requests. **Re-read 2026-10-19, unchanged:** the changelog's newest entry is still that of 2026-10-07, and the Architect's run of TZ-23's own instrument against the venue that day wrote the first look's six files byte for byte as TZ-23 had, at `0.832` s a page and a worker in the morning; TZ-29's held fetch of the same markets ran at `0.363` an hour later, its slowest chunk `30.1` s, every chunk inside the idle stretch. **TZ-29's fetch on the host, 2026-10-20:** its walk `17.9` s, 202 chunks every one inside the idle stretch, the longest `25.8` s, `1,337` s of fetching projected after its first 30, `2,166` s of wall clock. **The maker rows, read by the Architect for TZ-31 on 2026-10-22** on M0's last 864 markets — exploration, no gate: `taker_only=false` serves each fill's maker rows beside its taker's, each with its own `proxy_wallet`, side, outcome, size and price, the maker's own; a market's maker rows are its full feed less its takers' rows as a multiset, ``2.64` to `3.52`` full rows a taker row by chunk; in a transaction the makers' sizes sum to the taker's at 1,150,934 of 1,151,005, and where they do not the venue prints a maker's size at two decimals and the taker's at six; no transaction carries two timestamps; and a taker's price is its one maker's price, or the size-weighted mean of several, at 135,854 of 135,864 rows on 120 markets, the other 10 differing below `1e-6`. Re-read 2026-10-22: the changelog's newest entries, of 2026-10-21, add equity prices and touch nothing here. **TZ-31's instrument on the venue, by the Architect on 2026-10-22:** on a stand-in of 1,008 markets read by TZ-29, `1792149300` … `1792451400`, its walk `15.9` s for 51 gamma requests; 336 chunks of three markets and both feeds, every one inside the idle stretch, the longest `14.7` s; `1,284` s of fetching projected after its first 30, `1,517.7` s of fetching at `0.511` s a page and a worker over `5,936` pages, `3,240` s of wall clock; at five markets a chunk over the formation's 864, `0.518` s a page and a worker and the longest chunk `22.5` s |

RTDS still served all four of the recorder's topics on 2026-10-04 (TZ-21's G-PATH), and none of the changelog's entries through 2026-10-07 names an end date. **The recorder's settlement feed rests on a legacy socket**; this project holds no credential (§6, egress), and a move is a TZ of its own. | **RTDS's fast prices, read live by the Architect on 2026-10-27** | from a second host, for ten seconds, 15 frames: `crypto_prices` `btcusdt` publishes one price a second, its payload stamped on the whole second and the message about `130` to `150` ms after it; `crypto_prices_chainlink` one a second, its payload stamped on the second before. A price sampled once a second on the second cannot withdraw a quote inside the 150 ms taker delay. **Binance's public trade archive**, `data.binance.vision`, served that host the daily aggregate trades of BTCUSDT spot, stamped in microseconds, and of the USDⓈ-M perpetual, in milliseconds, each with its `.CHECKSUM`; `api.binance.com` answered `451` there. The capture host's reach to either is unread |
| **maker incentives** | **Re-read 2026-10-09, and unchanged on 2026-10-19** by the venue's fee and maker-rebate pages: makers pay no fee; the Crypto maker rebate is 20% of each market's taker fees, paid daily in pUSD and fee-curve weighted — each filled maker order earns the market's pool in proportion to `C × 0.07 × p × (1 − p)` at its own price, totals per market, a `1` pUSD minimum payout; and since 2026-05-28 a taker rebate programme returns up to 50% of a taker's own fees by 30-day volume tier, to takers only. Read 2026-09-12: the measured `rebateRate` `0.2` is a share of the taker fee paid on a fill; separately the venue runs a liquidity-rewards programme paying for resting orders near the midpoint, scored quadratically in distance and linearly in size, sampled once a minute, configured per market as `rewards.rewardsMinSize`, `rewards.rewardsMaxSpread` and `clobRewards[].rewardsDailyRate`. **Measured TZ-07a V9 and TZ-14 §3.8:** the `rewards` object is absent from every `gamma.json` read — 400, then 1,200 — but the same documents carry `rewardsMaxSpread` `4.5`, `rewardsMinSize` `50` and `holdingRewardsEnabled` `False` as top-level keys at 1,200 of 1,200, beside `feesEnabled`, `takerBaseFee` `1000`, `makerBaseFee` `1000` and `makerRebatesFeeShareBps` `10000`. **No top-level key carries a daily rate**, so no maker income can be computed from the capture. **Measured against the venue's payouts on 2026-10-20** (§4): the `MAKER_REBATE` paid near 00:45 UTC is `0.19975` to `0.20003` of `Σ 0.07 × C × p × (1 − p)` over a wallet's maker fills of the previous UTC day, at four wallet-days of three wallets — the operative field is `rebateRate`, and `makerRebatesFeeShareBps` `10000`, `makerBaseFee` and `takerBaseFee` `1000` are not; **no liquidity rewards on these markets since August's programme:** a large maker's `REWARD` payouts end on 2026-09-06, and `rewards/markets/{conditionId}` is empty for three open markets that carry `rewardsMinSize` `50` and `rewardsMaxSpread` `4.5`. M0's NO EARN holds for any rebate share up to `0.213536` (§4). |
| host is shared | unrelated production services live on the same filesystem — `crypto-auto`, `my_real_estate_bot`, `seahome_webapp.git`, `/var/www`, `/var/log`. The recorder never deletes anything it did not write. |
| capture path | `/var/lib/btc-recorder/**`, for the chain book `/var/lib/btc-chainbook/**`, for the pair book `/var/lib/btc-pairbook/**` (§2.5) and for the reward book `/var/lib/btc-rewardbook/**` (§2.6) — all outside the repository by design, so no capture can reach git history |

| **RTDS session limit, measured 2026-09-10** | the server closes every websocket `7,200` s after it opens, `1001 Going away`, timer restarting on each new connection. Undocumented. **No TZ may require an unbroken socket for longer than this.** |
| clock | NTP-disciplined; SNTP replies validated at reception since `4216c04`. Maximum absolute offset measured over the TZ-05a window: `18.789` ms; **over TZ-14's 1,200 intervals: `33.946` ms**, no interval above 50. An offset above 50 ms invalidates a latency claim; it is not a membership condition for anything. |
| **sandbox limit — network** | calls that read the git token and send it to `api.github.com` are refused. `git` push/pull work. Release creation and asset upload cannot be done from inside the session — hand the upload to the Boss and verify by anonymous download. **The Architect's session is not the Executor's and is not constant:** it could not reach `github.com` on 2026-09-22 (TZ-18 §10 C3) and reached it and both venue hosts on 2026-09-23. A TZ never depends on the Architect's reach; a check it cannot perform becomes a BLOCK condition (CANON hard rule 13). |
| **GitHub Releases — the archive's destination, read 2026-10-10 and 2026-10-11** | GitHub's documentation, read by the Architect on 2026-10-10 and 2026-10-11: up to `1,000` assets a Release; each file under 2 GiB, `2,147,483,648` bytes; no limit on a Release's total size or its bandwidth; unauthenticated API requests limited to `60` an hour from one address; content-creating requests to `80` a minute and `500` an hour, a refusal `403` or `429` with `retry-after` or `x-ratelimit-reset`, else a wait of at least a minute, growing. **The API's record of an asset carries its digest:** on 2026-10-11 the Architect read `GET /repos/seahomebatumi-ai/btc-5m-twap/releases/tags/tz-01a-dataset` and that Release's asset list through a second route, because its own session's proxy refused `api.github.com` for this repository — Release `385702977`, one asset, `twap-divergence-observations.parquet`, `size` `76818669`, `state` `uploaded`, `digest` `sha256:229a944f…` equal to §2.1's, `browser_download_url` `https://github.com/seahomebatumi-ai/btc-5m-twap/releases/download/tz-01a-dataset/twap-divergence-observations.parquet` — and downloaded the asset through TZ-27's own `download_checked`: `200`, 76,818,669 bytes, SHA-256 equal, in 1.4 to 3.6 s, github.com answering with a `302` to `release-assets.githubusercontent.com`. **Writing a Release needs a credential the session cannot send** (above; contract §5.1): TZ-27's B-UPLOAD is the Boss's block, under the token the primary checkout's remote URL carries or else the one `gh` holds, and its reads of the Release are anonymous, 120 s after its upload completes because an anonymous reply may be cached for 60 s. Its requests carry `User-Agent: btc-5m-twap-tz27` and `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28`. **TZ-27's Release `409534533`** on `tz-27-pairbook-streams` holds 51 assets, `678,739,504` bytes, every one downloaded anonymously and hashed equal by the Architect on 2026-10-11 in 11 s; the Architect's session could not read the API's record of it. **The host's rates, by TZ-27:** B-UPLOAD's 51 uploads, `678,739,504` bytes, from 08:24:29 to 08:25:42 UTC with 1 s between writes, about `9,297,801` bytes a second; `verify`'s 51 downloads and its API reads from 08:27:46 to 08:28:34, about `14,140,406`. TZ-28 budgets a transfer at `2,000,000` bytes a second, under a quarter of the slower, and sends every request through TZ-27's `build_request`. **By TZ-28's first run:** its upload of `430,047,476` bytes ran at seconds 195 to 235 of one window with the 1 s gaps, about `10.2` MB a second, and its downloads at seconds 358 to 391, about `13` MB a second; one idle stretch carries a day's streams at these rates. **TZ-30's downloads:** every stream file by its Release's download path, `https://github.com/seahomebatumi-ai/btc-5m-twap/releases/download/<tag>/<asset>`, anonymous, `User-Agent: btc-5m-twap-tz30` and `Accept: application/octet-stream`, github.com answering `302` to `release-assets.githubusercontent.com`; the Architect downloaded TZ-27's 51 assets so on 2026-10-20, `678,739,504` bytes, each equal to its set's bytes and SHA-256, in under a second each. The same session is refused the Release pages and the API for this repository, so the per-asset sizes of the later Releases are read on the host, from each run's `set.json`. **The archives verify anonymously, so the repository stays public:** on 2026-10-29 it served no Release without a token from between 07:48 and 08:02 UTC to about 09:00 UTC, and both archives' runs deferred in that hour (§7 item 128). **The reward book's archive's first Release, `427629503`**, 86 assets, `890,975,956` bytes, was downloaded anonymously by the Architect on 2026-10-29 through `github.com`'s download path, 86 of 86 equal to the set's bytes and SHA-256, in 59.5 s. |
| **sandbox limit — destructive actions** | `kill` is refused by the permission classifier, alone or inside a compound command, and TZ-09 established that `rm -rf` of several trees is refused the same way. `rm -rf` of **one** tree was refused for TZ-12, TZ-13 and TZ-15 and **not refused for TZ-14**, three refusals in four TZs, and for two of TZ-16a's three trees while the third went through, so refusal is not predictable. **Any TZ that must restart the recorder or remove a tree keeps the fallback: it hands the Boss one exact block** and verifies the outcome from `runtime.jsonl`, `ps`, `df` and `test -e` rather than from his report. TZ-18's service was the first process a TZ started that had to be stopped this way: the Boss ran its block, and TZ-18a §0.4 verified the `stop` record. **TZ-18a's `rm -rf` of three trees, one tree per command, was not refused.** TZ-18a's service, now `btc-chainbook.service`, is stopped only by K-20C (§2.5). **TZ-20 tried `systemctl` against a unit:** `enable --now` was refused as "Unauthorized Persistence", and the Boss ran both starts; `install`, `daemon-reload`, `systemd-analyze verify`, `systemctl kill`, three `git worktree remove --force` and three `rm -rf`, one tree each, were not refused. **TZ-21:** B-MOVE's `git checkout` in the recorder's unit tree and B-KILL-REC's `systemctl kill --signal=SIGTERM` were not refused; three of B-RECLAIM-SCRATCH's ten single-tree `rm -rf`, identical in form, were — "[Interfere With Workloads]" — and the Boss ran them, verified by `test -e`. `systemctl restart` has not been tried. **TZ-26:** B-SWITCH — `systemctl disable --now` and `enable --now` — was refused as "[Production Deploy]" and run by the Boss, 25 minutes later and 10 s before its band (§7 item 104); B-INSTALL's `install`, `daemon-reload` and `systemd-analyze verify`, B-KILL-SVC's `systemctl kill --signal=SIGTERM` and every `rm -rf`, one tree each, were not refused. **TZ-27:** one of two single-tree `rm -rf` and the instrument's `delete` were refused as "[Irreversible Local Destruction]", and a plain `df`/`du` right after a refusal as "[Auto-Mode Bypass]"; the Boss ran each. `systemctl kill`, the tag's push and every other mode were not refused. **TZ-28:** B-ENABLE's `systemctl enable --now` was refused as "[Auto-Mode Bypass]" and run by the Boss; B-INSTALL's `install`, `daemon-reload` and `systemd-analyze verify`, both `rm -rf` of B-RECLAIM-SCRATCH and B-RECLAIM-OWN's lines were not refused. **TZ-29 and TZ-30:** every `rm -rf`, one tree each, and every `git worktree remove` were not refused. **TZ-33:** `I reclaim` of four earlier session directories was refused with no reason given, and B-ENABLE's `systemctl enable --now` as "[Unauthorized Persistence]"; the Boss ran both (§7 item 124). B-INSTALL's `install`, `daemon-reload` and `systemd-analyze verify`, `systemctl kill`, and B-RECLAIM-OWN's `reclaim`, `git worktree remove` and `rm -rf` were not refused. **TZ-34:** B-RECLAIM-SCRATCH's `reclaim` and its three single-tree `rm -rf`, B-RETRY's `systemctl start --no-block` and B-RECLAIM-OWN's lines were run by the Executor and not refused, and B-INSTALL's seven lines exited `0` in the session's own log; B-ENABLE was the Boss's by its own text, carrying the credential's proof. |
| **harness — background output** | Claude Code keeps its own copy of each background command's terminal output under the session's own directory, `/tmp/claude-0/-root-btc-5m-twap/<session>/tasks/`, beside the scratchpad; no step of the running session can remove it. The next TZ's B-RECLAIM-SCRATCH removes it with the session's directory, once no running `claude` process made that directory (§7 items 91 and 95). TZ-23's removed the three directories TZ-21 had left, TZ-24's TZ-23's pair, TZ-26's TZ-24's, TZ-27's TZ-26's, one by the Boss's hand, TZ-28's TZ-27's, TZ-29's TZ-28's, TZ-30's TZ-29's, with three directories no report names, and TZ-31's TZ-30's, with `eff3ddd1…`, and TZ-32's TZ-31's, with `6e88615c…` and `f9a43b8e…`, and TZ-33's TZ-32's, with `54afa9d7…` and `bda03c0e…`, by the Boss's hand and out of its order (§7 item 124); TZ-33's session left three, `0358b0b2…`, `621e47cc…` and `b82e3547…`, the next TZ's — unless a reboot emptied `/tmp`. **What a session prints, Claude Code keeps:** its transcript under `/root/.claude/` holds every command's terminal output, so a read the Executor runs is written outside the work tree, and a TZ's print prohibitions bind those reads (§7 item 118). **Since 2026-10-28 the Executor's session is spawned by a remote-control `claude` process** — `claude remote-control --name BTC-5M-TWAP-VPS --spawn=same-dir`, under tmux session `crypto-rc` — and runs as `claude.exe --print --sdk-url …`; it makes two directories at its start, its own and one beside it, and one more at each restart of its container, which loses its background waits (TZ-33 report §2.15); **a block handed to the Boss runs in his login and ends with it** (§7 item 124) |

Not yet decided by any TZ and therefore not present: the Parquet decision journal and any deployment of
the engine. The capture units are the recorder's, TZ-20's, the pair book's, TZ-26's, and the reward book's, TZ-33's,
and all three run (above); the chain book's is disabled, the pair book's fallback; the archives' timers are TZ-28's and
TZ-34's. **Capture retention is fixed by TZ-09 §5:** the capture is never
deleted; archiving is its own reversible TZ, and moves the capture verbatim to a Release asset that the Release's
digest and a download prove byte-identical before the file leaves the host — TZ-27, 2026-10-11, was the first, for
the pair book's streams, and TZ-28 puts the same steps on a daily timer, and TZ-34 the reward book's on a timer of their
own (§2.6); a TZ's `/root/tz<NN>-work/` is reclaimed by the next TZ
once that TZ's report is on `main`, except what a committed artifact names by hash, which is copied
to `/root/btc-forensics/` first.

Repaired by rule: **a hunk map is stated at `-U0`**; and item 72's rule covers the rest — what the host holds is read on the host by the TZ that states it. |
| 80 | **TZ-18a's as-of instant fell below a file its own stopping rule had seen.** R3 stopped at the first minute-check at which `E2 = 1790187000`'s manifest existed and set `A = floor(now)`; the recorder had written that manifest in the same second, so its `st_mtime_ns` exceeded `A · 10^9` and D12 excluded it. G-TIERC reached PASS at its minimum on `1790185200` and `1790186100` — the first from a window that was considered and fully loaded though it qualified for no G-DEPLOY unit — and `1790187000`, fully loaded, is unread. `test -e` reads nothing, so the exclusion was blind and biased nothing, but it cost the interlock its third unit. The Architect's defect. | closed by ruling — PASS stands as the rule reads it, and the interlock continues in TZ-16a (§2.5), as TZ-16 wrote it. Repaired by rule: **an as-of instant is never earlier than an event its stopping condition observed — the check's clock is rounded up, not down — and the proof runs only once the clock is past it.** |
| 79 | **The TZ-18a report states two figures of its first commit as its last's, and one statistic wrongly.** The file is 1,683 lines and `69,517` bytes at `ca4bbc1`; `69,357` is `9973e44`'s size. The `-U0` diff against `8caa0b4` is 99 blocks at `ca4bbc1`; 97 is `9973e44`'s count, and two of the 99 cross a D-item's stated range — base 75–77, where D6's line 75 meets D3's 76 and 77, and 360–365, two blank lines past D6's 363. And "within 30 ms, 20 of 21": every one of the 21 skews is within 30 ms, and 20 are within 28 ms. The report also never prints R0's count; the instrument prints its V-lines only after all 39 asserts pass, so R0's printed V5 and V7 imply 39 of 39, and the TZ fixed R0's count in §6 with no V-row to carry it. The Executor's defects, with the Architect's share on R0. | closed by ruling — the hash anchors the file, no figure moves a reading, and the committed report is not edited. Repaired by rule, item 74's extended: **after any new commit, every figure a report states about its artifact is recomputed from the head it names**; and **every run whose outcome a TZ fixes has a V-row.** |
| 78 | **TZ-18a's Executor fixed forward past a BLOCK clause.** §3.4 read "A failed assert in steps 1–7 is BLOCKED, and the service is never started." The first commit, `9973e44`, built `base_rate` with `T0 = p − 300` included — 2,017 slots — and its own assert stopped `--prestart` at step 3, before any manifest was opened or any request made. The Executor pushed `ca4bbc1`, which changes that one expression — 6 insertions and 2 deletions, re-derived by the Architect — ran R1 again and started the service. The report lists it among what could not be implemented as written and does not name the clause it bypassed. The Executor's defect, and it moved no number: no live request ran under `9973e44`, and every gated figure comes from `ca4bbc1` after its push. | closed by ruling — accepted, the diff between the two commits read by the Architect. Repaired by rule: **a clause that reads BLOCKED binds whatever caused the failure, the Executor's own code included; a TZ that wants a defect fixable inside the session says so, before its first venue request only, and requires the copy and R0 repeated on the new commit and both commits disclosed.** |
| 21 | The TZ-07a report's §2.5 summary carries one `eligible bins` column for two estimators whose eligibility differs — 10 / 9 / 6 / 5 / 2 / 2 uncorrected against 8 / 10 / 9 / 7 / 3 / 2 corrected. The per-tau tables are correct; only the summary is ambiguous, and G2 counts failures over eligible bins. | closed by rule: **a summary table reporting two estimators reports eligibility per estimator.** The committed report is not edited. |

## 8. What does not exist yet

No validated link — Phase 1 can only fail to disqualify one — no measured edge, no Polymarket
client code, no execution path, no capital at risk. Nothing in this repository can place an order.
The recorder reads; it cannot write to any venue.

Two pricers exist. `p_fair`, the normal link, fails its scale gate at `tau` 60 and 30 on two
disjoint sets, and TZ-10b measured why — the settlement residual is not normal, and less so the
smaller `tau` is. The Student pricer, fitted on the Thursday–Friday set and frozen, **is not
disqualified at `tau` 240 … 60 inside its domain**, by a gate whose false-failure probability and
power were measured before any outcome it judged was read, on 750 Tuesday-to-Thursday slots. That is
the most Phase 1 can say, and it says nothing about the market.

The weekend is the other finding. Causal volatility there runs at about a third of the weekday
level, the domain rule declines to quote on more than nine weekend intervals in ten, and every
constant this project has seen fail to transfer failed between a weekday set and that weekend. **No
set scored under the sized gate contains a weekend.**

**The market has been read three times and scored twice.** TZ-14 opened 16,800 order-book replies over 1,200
intervals and found the capture complete, the book one tick wide at the median, takeable at the venue's
minimum size, emptying toward the close — two-sided at every read four minutes out and at 60% of them
one minute out — and a round trip that costs `4.1` probability points four minutes out and `1.9` one
minute out, because the fee falls as the market makes up its mind. TZ-15 then took, on paper, one share
at each of 1,924 checkpoints where the Student pricer called the ask cheap after the fee: the side it
chose won less often than the ask implied at four of five `tau`, no `tau` reads EDGE, and 1,200 slots
gave no power to say NO EDGE. After the fact the outcomes tracked the market's prices rather than the
pricer's at four of five `tau`, and the market's mid beat `p_t` on the Brier score at every `tau`.
**TZ-16a then read 2,400 slots no one had seen and closed four `tau` of five:** at 180, 120, 90 and 60
the side the pricer calls cheap after the fee wins about as often as its ask implies and less often than
the pricer claims, beyond the lower tail of the pricer's own law, and loses after the fee. At 240 it is
undecided, and stays so: the second look is withdrawn. **The market is not wrong where this pricer says
it is, at four horizons of five.**

**The backup track has its structure, and its first reading: VIOLATION.** The fifteen-minute market and the
five-minute market inside it settle on one published number, so their outcomes nest exactly, and a
violation of the nesting on executable quotes needs no pricer. From 2026-09-23 17:39:50 UTC the chain book recorded
both markets' books, within 30 ms of one another at every checkpoint of its proof, and Tier C held under its load,
twice. **TZ-24, B2, read them** up to the window whose five-minute market is M0's last member: at 7 of 1,079 windows
the books offered the pair that pays at least 1 for less than 1 after both fees, inside one taker delay — about
`1.9` USDC at the touch over eleven days. **A violation measured is not an edge captured.** Whether a taker could
have had one — through the 150 ms delay, at depth, both legs together, with the strikes known live — is B3's
question, and seven snapshots a window cannot answer it; TZ-26's pair book streams every change of the four books
instead, since 2026-10-10 19:30:06 UTC. TZ-27 moved its first streams to a Release by hand, and TZ-28's timer has
moved them every day since 2026-10-11. **TZ-30 read them and closed the track: NO CAPTURE** — on the first 768 qualifying windows
from 2026-10-11 08:15 UTC, each replayed through the 150 ms delay at depth, both legs, with the strikes known live, every
block of 96 lost; the violations are gone, on one leg at least, before a taker's order matches.

**The maker side is measured, and closed for its window.** Taking a quote and paying the fee lost at four horizons
of five; the maker track asked whether resting quotes, which pay no fee and collect a fifth of it as rebate, earn at
settlement inside a window fixed in advance — first on the venue's public trade history (M0), then on the live
book in simulation (M1), then live at minimum size (M2). **TZ-23, M0's first look, read UNDECIDABLE:** on 4,032
markets no TZ had scored, the makers earned `0.211` c a share inside `[T0, T0 + 240)`, of it `0.224` c rebate —
before the rebate, about nothing. **TZ-29, the second look, read NO EARN over both looks:** on 8,064 markets the
makers kept `0.231` c a share, `0.219` c of it rebate — under half a tick a share with the rebate, and the rebate is
the venue's 20%, measured against its payouts. M1 and M2 do not open. **The W track asks what the pool hid** (CANON
PART II): whether the makers who earned most on markets already read keep earning on markets whose fills no one has
read — TZ-31, W0, on the first 1,008 after `1792451400`. **They do: W0 read W-EARN**, the ten named makers `0.828` c a
share, rebate included. **A quote of ours at the touch does not: W1 read W1-NO-EARN** (TZ-32) — first in every queue under
half a tick a share, all of it rebate, and behind it a loss. W2 does not open, and the W track is closed. **The L track
asks it again of a quote the venue pays to rest** — its liquidity rewards, `148,331` pUSD a day over 14,089 markets on
2026-10-27 (CANON §1.7) — on markets where information arrives slowly; **its capture, the reward book, runs since 2026-10-28 11:30:16 UTC
(TZ-33)**, its streams archived daily since 2026-10-29 (TZ-34), and nothing of it is measured yet.

**Both captures run as systemd system units** since 2026-10-04 07:39 UTC, outside every session: the recorder,
on `4216c04` with both tiers, had stopped on 2026-10-02 at about 11:36 UTC after 20 days, and the chain book on
2026-10-03 at 08:56:15 UTC, both with the sessions they had been started from (§7 item 85), and TZ-20 restarted
both. Since TZ-21's restart at 22:22:07 UTC that evening the recorder keeps two hours of runtime records instead
of all of them, every slice unchanged (§6, memory). Since 2026-10-10 19:30:06 UTC the pair book has run in the
chain book's place (TZ-26), and since 2026-10-11 the archive's timer has moved its streams off the host every day
(TZ-28); since 2026-10-28 11:30:16 UTC the reward book has streamed each day's reward markets beside them (TZ-33), and since
2026-10-29 its archive's timer has moved its streams off the host every day (TZ-34). `/dev/vda2` held `12,298,153,984` bytes free at TZ-27's read before its removal on 2026-10-11, the lowest
reading to date, and `12,947,021,824` after TZ-28's §9. **The consumer is
stopped, measured at
thirty-six reads across twenty-six days**,
and headroom is **`58` days** on §6's figure, conditional on that service staying disabled; the archive's daily runs keep
the pair book's streams far below their cap and floor (§6, headroom).
Between them the pricers, the recorder, the chain book, the pair book and the venue's own documents have produced
nineteen conclusions — the Phase 0 answer, the normal link's Phase 1 answer, the reason for it, the shape of the residual, the
closure of the Student pricer by an unsized gate, the size of the gate, the Student pricer's Phase 1
answer under it, the inventory of the book, Phase 2's first reading, undecided, Phase 2's first decisive
look, NO EDGE at four `tau`, the backup track's first
and second answers, the identity of the reading both families settle on, M0's first look, undecided,
B2's reading, VIOLATION, M0's reading over both looks, NO EARN, B3's, NO CAPTURE, W0's, W-EARN, and W1's, W1-NO-EARN —
five deployment proofs, TZ-33's of L0's capture a sixth, TZ-34's of its archive a seventh, and
one deployment that failed on its own build, the measurements of the feed and of the host that §2 and §6
record, and eight written predictions that the data refuted:
the third on its mechanism rather than its answer, the fourth on one row of five, the fifth on four rows
of six and, at `tau = 60`, on its direction, the sixth on two rows of seven — the book's two-sidedness
and the cost at the short horizons — and the seventh on two rows of five: the long shot's share of the
selection at `tau = 60`, and the gate's power to close anywhere; the eighth on two rows of five, the
same two `tau` read the other way — NO EDGE at 90, where TZ-15's set had sat far from the lower tail, and
not at 240, where it had sat beyond it.
**Three specifications were stopped by the Executor before any measurement existed**, and the eight
mechanical checks of CANON PART VI exist because of them. Every TZ written under those checks ran
until TZ-16, which its Executor stopped at step 4 on an expectation its C2 had classed as quoted when
no instrument had printed it (§7 item 82); and each audit still found defects of the Architect's —
eight in TZ-18 alone (§7 items 75 and 76), five in TZ-18a, none moving a reading (§7 items 79 to 81),
and six in TZ-16 (§7 items 82 and 83), and four in TZ-16a, none moving a number (§7 item 84), and three in TZ-21, none moving a reading (§7 items 91 to
93), and three in TZ-23, among them a memory floor taken on a seventh of its population, none moving a reading (§7
items 97 to 99), and in TZ-24 its `reclaim` wording again, among nine departures by TZ-24's Executor, all upheld
(§7 item 102), and in TZ-26 a band its block did not guard and an instrument that printed no rate, among nine
departures by TZ-26's Executor, all upheld (§7 items 103 to 105), and a deadline for the archive taken from the
wrong bound (§7 item 106), and in TZ-27 a count of V16's worded for the wrong store and transfers timed to no band,
among nine departures by its Executor, all upheld (§7 items 107 and 109), and in TZ-28 a report item its own
prohibition forbade, among ten departures by its Executor, all upheld (§7 items 110 and 111), and for TZ-29 a
projection in its delivery chat that named one of the two quantities it depends on, beside six departures by its
Executor, all upheld (§7 items 116 and 117), and for TZ-30 a print prohibition its Executor's own read broke once,
among seven departures (§7 item 118), and eight departures by TZ-31's Executor, all upheld (§7 item 119), and in TZ-32 a
memory claim that held for its replay and not for its score, among eleven departures by its Executor, all upheld (§7
items 122 and 123), and in TZ-33 two runs of the archive its own systemd steps set off and two blocks handed to the
Boss that its text could not hold, among nine departures by its Executor, all upheld (§7 items 124 to 126), and in
TZ-34 a number its specification shared with another, among fifteen departures by its Executor, all upheld (§7 items 127
and 130). A gate that
could close its question in one direction only is why TZ-16a reads
each answer from its own exact test; a deployment that failed and could not be stopped is why a TZ
that starts a process now proves its request path before it starts and names its stop (CANON hard
rule 14); a block run outside its band is why a block timed to a band now waits for it in its own text (the same
rule); a transfer that overlapped the stream is why the archive's every request now waits for the idle stretch
(§7 item 109); and a fix pushed past a BLOCK clause is why that clause now binds whatever caused the
failure (§7 item 78); and a hand-over that outlived the Boss's login, and another whose order no record kept, are
why a block now goes to him only inside its band, in one line, appending to its log (§7 item 124).
